# PROGRESS — archive

> Superseded session entries and boot proofs, newest first. **Not read by default** — `docs/PROGRESS.md`
> is the live ledger. Grep here when you need the history of a decision or a closed phase.
> Split out 2026-08-15 (the file had grown to 417 KB and was re-read in full every session).

- **This session (2026-08-19): P8.10.B SPLIT into B1/B2, and P8.10.B1 — `lead` gains `phone` — BUILT.**
  - **Why split (owner ASK):** B's own tasks named three things — the phone field, duplicate detection, spam scoring — and the P8.10 split note had already measured the phone change at ~20 files across five projects. B1/B2 map exactly onto B's two "Done when" clauses, so no criterion is orphaned. B1 also carries a net-new **dependency**, B2 a net-new **audit mounting** in content-service (which has none today) — the honest reason they are separate sessions.
  - **Owner decisions:** phone is **REQUIRED on both website forms** (a Gulf agency loses most inbound without a callable number), and it gets a **real dial-code picker** on `libphonenumber-js`, not a text box — a typo'd number is caught at submit time instead of at call time. Stored **E.164**; the DB column is nullable only because pre-B1 leads have none.
  - **One validator, one formatter, one rule (U3).** `libs/contracts/src/lib/common/phone/` is the whole decision: `phoneSchema` validates **per-country**, transforms to E.164, and rejects with the catalog KEY `validation.invalidPhone` — which `ZodValidationPipe` already renders in the caller's locale, so U24 fell out with **zero** new plumbing. The same `formatPhoneInternational` renders the admin cell AND the team email, so the inbox and the email cannot disagree. The schema deliberately **refuses to guess a country server-side**: a bare national number is rejected, and only the picker composes.
  - **No dataset to maintain.** Countries + calling codes come from libphonenumber's own metadata, names from a cached `Intl.DisplayNames` in the reader's locale, flags from the ISO-2 code's regional-indicator codepoints.
  - **The verifier found 4 real defects; all fixed.** The load-bearing one: the field group is `dir="ltr"` (a phone number is LTR in both locales) — which had swallowed the **country search box and the localized country names**, so an Arabic visitor typed `السعودية` into an LTR field on the Arabic-first site. The popover BOX now stays in the LTR flow (so `inset-inline-start` still anchors it under the trigger) while its CONTENT takes the page direction back. Two specs added; probed — removing the `dir` fails them. The other three were stale docs (`website.md`, `ui-kit.md`, `dashboard.md`).
  - **A 5th defect only the screenshot could find (U21e).** The picker opened on **Iceland** — `activeIndex` 0 of 245 names sorted alphabetically — so the visitor never saw their own country. It now opens ON the selected country and scrolls it into view, exposed as `aria-activedescendant` so a spec can assert it. No test would have caught this; looking did.
  - **Proven by probe, not assumed:** dropping the `00`-prefix handling and giving the schema a default country each fail their own spec; the RTL assertion fails when the `dir` is removed.
  - **Deliberate gate deviation:** the verifier ran with the app stack down but **postgres UP**, so the `*_test` int specs EXECUTED instead of self-skipping — the exact false-green P8.10.A was bitten by. No Nx runs in the postgres container, so OPS5 is not in play.
  - **U26 baseline unchanged at 566/566** — all four new folders born compliant.
  - **Closed:** verifier PASS (21 projects, every `*_test` int spec EXECUTED) + boot-verifier **UP** (12/12, `RestartCount=0`, 110 routes, no N12 ESM failure from the new dep). Runtime-proven: `+966 50 123 4567` → stored `+966501234567` in the DB and on the outbox payload; omitted / bare-national / garbage all 400 with genuine ar vs en text; team + ack emails dispatched with `href="tel:+966501234567"`; `GET /leads?search=501234567` finds it. **Looked at** (Playwright + system Chrome on nginx:8190): both forms × ar+en × phone+desktop with the picker open — zero RTL overflow — and the admin inbox in ar/dark + en/light at desktop + tablet.
  - **NEXT:** `/continue-plan` → **P8.10.B2 — duplicate detection + lead spam scoring** (`submissionCount` + `lastSubmittedAt` + an audit row instead of a second lead; mounts `@sanad/audit` in content-service).

- **This session (2026-08-19, owner ASK → launch resequencing): P8.10 SPLIT A/B/C, and P8.10.A — gateway edge hardening — BUILT. The owner wants the site public soon and can't work on this daily, so we ship only what a _marketed_ site needs, deploy, then continue the rest against the live system.**
  - **Route to launch (decided today):** P8.10.A → P8.10.B (`lead.phone` + duplicates) → P8.23.A (consent + pixels) → P8.23.B (legal pages + WhatsApp) → P9.A/P9.B (deploy) → advertise. **Deferred past launch:** P8.9, P8.11, P8.12, P8.13–P8.22 — contracts/proposals handled outside the platform until P8.15–P8.18. **P8.10.C (honeypot + time-floor + Turnstile) folded into P8.23**: it only earns its keep once paid traffic starts, and it degrades open. **Duplicate leads → `submissionCount` + an audit row, NOT a `lead_activity` table** — the activity timeline is P8.15 `crm-service`'s (`08-client-intake` §5.1), so building one here would create a concept that phase must then own or migrate.
  - **The security bug this phase found.** `clientIp()` read the **first** `x-forwarded-for` hop, but nginx _appends_ — so that hop is caller-supplied and any limiter keyed on it is bypassable by rotating the header. Precedence inverted to `req.ip` under `trust proxy` (`TRUST_PROXY_HOPS`: **1** = our nginx, **2** behind Cloudflare — **P9 must set this deliberately**). The same bug left `request_id` **always null** on HTTP-originated audit rows. The derivation is now one shared `requestContextFromHttp` in `@sanad/util`, used by the gateway middleware _and_ `@sanad/audit`, so a 429 and its trail cannot disagree about the caller.
  - **A bespoke guard, not `@nestjs/throttler`.** `ThrottlerModule.forRoot` resolves limits once at bootstrap (incompatible with settings-fed limits) and has no fail-open model — a throwing storage becomes a 500. ~130 lines, zero new runtime dep. Limits come from the new **`security` settings group** on a 60s TTL with **synchronous** stale-while-revalidate (awaiting would put an RMQ round trip on the hot path once a minute). Fails open four ways: Redis rejects; Redis **hangs** (a 100ms race — `CacheModule` sets `maxRetriesPerRequest: null`, so a dead Redis _queues forever_); settings unreachable (last-good snapshot, never back to defaults); ip unknown.
  - **Proven by probe, not assumed.** Removing the clamping → 3 specs fail; reset-on-failure → the last-good spec fails; no TTL guard → the once-per-60s spec fails; removing the deadline race → the hang spec **times out**, which is the production symptom. One probe caught a hole in my own harness: the guard spec built _two_ request objects, so the spoof test passed against a deliberately spoofable guard. One object now; the test bites.
  - **`security` is the first non-`branding` settings group** and needed **zero admin component changes** — only labels. `SETTINGS_DEFAULTS` moved out of `branding-catalog.ts` into `settings-defaults/`, with a spec asserting no cross-group key collision (the spread would silently shadow).
  - **The verifier found 4 real defects; all fixed, none argued with.** (1) `useBodyParser` calls `this.use()` **immediately** — parsers registered before helmet/context meant malformed-JSON and 413 responses short-circuited to Express's `finalhandler`: no envelope, no helmet headers, **no `X-Request-Id`**. Order corrected, verified in `express-adapter.js` rather than assumed. (2) The bounds were only a gateway clamp, so an admin typing `0` was told **"Settings saved"** for a value that never applies — now a descriptor-declared range, rejected on write with `settings.value.outOfRange` (ar+en) and rendered as `min`/`max` on the control. (3) `contracts.md` + `util.md` stale (DOC1). (4) 429 now carries the `X-RateLimit-*` trio, not only `Retry-After`.
  - **Also:** helmet (CSP all-`none` — the gateway serves JSON, never a document; HSTS **off in dev** or it pins every localhost project to https); **CORS deny-by-default, empty outside dev** — the admin is same-origin `/api` with a Bearer header and the website is SSR, so nothing needs CORS today; 1MB JSON cap at nginx _and_ the gateway (media is presigned direct-to-R2, nothing multipart passes); nginx mints `X-Request-Id`, deliberately overwriting a client value (a caller-controlled string in your logs is an injection surface); **no `limit_req` in dev** so the settings-driven policy stays observable — noted for P9's prod nginx as an outer volumetric ring.
  - **U26 baseline unchanged at 566/566** — every new file born compliant. Full `run-many` green across 21 projects with the stack DOWN.
  - **Closed:** verifier PASS + boot-verifier **UP** (11/11 containers, `RestartCount=0`, 110 routes, no ESM failure from helmet/ioredis). Runtime-proven: flood `201×5 → 429` (ar+en), 9 forged `X-Forwarded-For` → **one** Redis bucket keyed on the real client, Redis stopped → **8/8 still 201** with the 100ms deadline logged, malformed JSON → N9 envelope **with** `X-Request-Id`, audit rows carrying the client ip + nginx's minted id. The stack-up run also caught a **stale int-spec assertion** that had been self-skipping with Postgres down (a branding query compared to the now-two-group catalog) — fixed and extended with a security-group case.
  - **Known, NOT introduced here:** the admin settings page overflows at 430px (branding **496**, security **507**) — pre-existing across every section, the shell's fixed 266px sidebar; recorded, not silently absorbed into this phase. Also `settings.events` has 0 consumers and accretes a message per write (a declared U27d orphan owned by P8.12).
  - **NEXT (superseded by the entry above):** P8.10.B, since split into B1/B2.

- **This session (2026-08-19): P8.8 CLOSED — verifier PASS + boot-verifier UP. The runtime gate it was never given found three defects that 38 green unit tests could not see, both fixed and pinned.**
  - **The audit interceptor never ran on RMQ.** `AuditModule` bound it with `APP_INTERCEPTOR`, and a spec asserted it landed in `ApplicationConfig.getGlobalInterceptors()` — true, and irrelevant: `connectMicroservice` builds the microservice with `new ApplicationConfig()` unless `inheritAppConfig` is passed (verified in `nest-application.js`), so a hybrid app's RMQ side inherits **no** global interceptor. Every write here arrives over RMQ, so real admin writes logged `actor_type: system` with a null ip. `inheritAppConfig: true` is NOT the fix — it would also hand the microservice the HTTP `ResponseEnvelopeInterceptor`, wrapping every RMQ reply. **`@Audited()` now binds its own interceptor**: identical on both transports, nothing for a `main.ts` to remember. `audited-rpc.spec.ts` drives a real handler over a real microservice transport and fails if the binding is dropped.
  - **"Append-only" did not cover TRUNCATE.** The guard was one `before update or delete … for each row` trigger, and row triggers never fire for TRUNCATE — the entire trail was erasable by one statement. Noticed because a row I had written 17 hours earlier was **gone** from the real settings DB while the setting it changed survived. Second `before truncate … for each statement` trigger added to `AUDIT_IMMUTABILITY_SQL` (settings migration `0005`); a `*_test` DB resets via the lib's `auditTestResetSql()` rather than improvising a disarm per spec.
  - **Every audited request wrote TWO rows** — the real one plus a phantom with null `entityId`/`before`/`after`, because the interceptor's guarantee row was emitted unconditionally. It is now a **fallback**: written only when the handler recorded nothing, or whenever the call failed (a `recordWithin` row rolls back with its transaction). Surfaced by boot-verifier, not by any unit test.
  - **The pattern behind all three: they are invisible to unit tests by construction.** Nothing in the repo compiled the Nest container, so a provider that could not resolve, an interceptor that never fired, and a duplicate row all passed 38 green tests. The two container-compile specs (`audit-module.spec.ts`, `audited-rpc.spec.ts`) exist to close that class, not these three instances.
  - **Proven at runtime, not asserted.** 8/8 containers up, settings-service boots once (it previously respawned 179× behind a Docker `RestartCount=0` that the supervisor absorbed); authenticated `PUT /api/settings` through nginx → HTTP 200 and **exactly one** `audit_log` row carrying actor + role `admin` + `actor_type: user` + ip `192.168.65.1` (the client's first `x-forwarded-for` hop, not nginx) + user-agent + request-id + before/after. On the real `settings` DB, all three of UPDATE / DELETE / TRUNCATE are refused with SQLSTATE 23001. Migrations 0000–0005 applied.
  - **Gate:** `nx run-many -t typecheck lint test build --parallel=2` green across **21 projects** with the stack DOWN; audit lib 46 tests (was 38), settings-service 50.
  - **NEXT:** `/continue-plan` → **P8.9 — Legal Entity Profile + configurable money (absorbs P8.6)**, which consumes `@sanad/money`: currency, symbol/placement, tax rate + label and the website's budget bands become resolved settings with coded defaults, and leads store a stable band code instead of a display label.

## Archived session entries

- **This session (2026-08-15, owner ASK — token spend · a swapping 16 GB Mac · uncommitted work · slow sessions, "without effecting the work quality"): PROCESS + INFRA re-architected. No feature scope touched; no phase checked off. Next phase is still P8.9.**
  - **Measured first.** Startup read **630 KB / ~155 K tokens**, and `verifier` + `boot-verifier` each re-read the same files → **~406 K tokens of boilerplate per phase**. Across 16 transcripts: 7,216 API calls, **1.83 B cache-read tokens, averaging 253 K context per call**. `PROGRESS.md` was 82% archive; **57% of the "binding law" was a change-log** — the thing U13 forbids, inside the file that declares U13.
  - **Context cut 75%: 594 KB → 150 KB (~152 K → ~38 K tokens).** Archives split out (`PROGRESS-archive.md`, `rules-changelog.md`), plan reads are phase-scoped, sub-agents get a distilled brief. Verified line-by-line: **0 non-blank lines lost, all 99 rule IDs intact**.
  - **Docs left `.claude/`** (now only `agents/` + `commands/`) for `docs/`, via `git mv` so history follows. 69 references across 26 files rewritten; repo-wide grep confirms zero dangling paths.
  - **New law: U31** (context is a budget), **OPS9** (one Docker VM shared by three projects — per-container caps, never the VM ceiling), **U12 amended** to two explicit tiers — the phase-**closing** gate is unchanged, only the mid-phase repeats are now `nx affected`. **N10 amended** with the poisoned-cache trap below.
  - **RAM.** 0 of 13 containers had any limit; all 13 now cap `mem_limit`, all 9 Node ones cap `NODE_OPTIONS`. Compose **profiles** added (`core` = 7 containers / 3 node, vs `full` = 13 / 9) — this required relaxing the gateway's and nginx's `depends_on`, because compose auto-enables a dependency's profile and would otherwise drag the whole stack up. `dev-up.sh` takes profiles and `--build` is now opt-in; watcher poll 400 ms → 1000 ms; root `.dockerignore` added; `nx --parallel=2` + Vitest `maxForks: 2` (the verifier spike is **host-side**, not Docker — OPS5 keeps the stack down for it).
  - **The false green, now fixed.** All nine `*.int.spec.ts` had **never run in CI** — no Postgres service, so each degraded to `describe.skip` and reported green. CI now runs `postgres:16-alpine` on the dev stack's port/credentials, seeds it from the init SQL, and sets `REQUIRE_DB_TESTS=1`; `failIfDbTestsRequired()` turns a skip into a failure. **Proven to bite:** with the flag set and no DB, the suite fails with the named error instead of passing.
  - **Trap worth keeping (N10).** The Nx cache can hold a **corrupt emit** and replay it forever: `libs/shared/auth` had a **0-byte `.d.ts`**, surfacing as TS6305/TS2306 in seven downstream projects while the source was fine. `--skip-nx-cache` does NOT fix it (it skips the write too, so the bad entry wins the next run) — only `nx reset` + rebuild does.
  - **Repo unblocked.** `tools/` and `libs/shared/{audit,money,sequence}` were untracked while committed `eslint.config.mjs`/`tsconfig.json` referenced them — `main` could not lint or typecheck from a clean clone. 241 files committed in 11 conventional chunks and pushed; full gate green.
  - **Not done, deliberately:** (a) the `.git` history purge (733 MB of stale `.pnpm-store` blobs) — disk-only, costs nothing in tokens/RAM/time, and a rewrite needs a force-push; see Open issues. (b) **Shrinking the Docker VM 7.75 → 6 GiB — planned, approved, then REVERSED on measurement.** The ceiling is a max, not a reservation (VM measured 0.55 GiB resident with two projects up), so with the containers capped it is never approached; and `full`'s limits sum to ~9.2 GiB, so 6 GiB would OOM-kill containers into a `restart: unless-stopped` loop — the exact thrash we removed. Caps are the mechanism; the ceiling stays default (OPS9).
  - **Boot proof (core profile).** `dev-up.sh` with no args → exactly **7 containers, all healthy**; `GET /api/health` through nginx:8190 returns `{success:true,data:{status:"ok"}}`. Every container now reports a REAL `MEM LIMIT` instead of `7.75GiB`. **Sanad total: 564 MiB** (gateway 172 · identity 192 · rabbit 142 · libs-builder 36 · postgres 36 · nginx 13 · redis 4); all three projects together 3.0 GiB of the VM. Host swap **15.5 GB → 13.5 GB with Sanad UP** (it was DOWN when the 15.5 was measured), and macOS released a swap file. Docker build cache pruned: **16.9 GB** reclaimed.
  - **NEXT:** `/continue-plan` → **P8.9 — Legal Entity Profile + configurable money (absorbs P8.6)**, sized per U0/E and split into sub-phases before any code.

- **This session (2026-08-09): P8.8 — platform primitives (money · sequence · audit) — BUILT in three sequentially-green stages, plus the request-context seam and the first real audited write path. The platform can now price a line, number a document, and prove who changed what from where — the three things every commercial phase after this one assumes exist.**
  - **`@sanad/money` (44 tests).** Integer **minor units + ISO code**, never a float. Framework-free on purpose — no Nest, no drizzle, no zod — so `apps/admin-portal`, `apps/website` and the P8.21 client portal render money through the SAME code the services price with (F8/F9); the shapes live in `@sanad/contracts` (U3). Ships `rounding` (4 explicit modes, symmetric about zero — JS `Math.round(-2.5) === -2` would make a refund round differently from the charge it reverses), `money-math` (incl. **`allocate`**, largest-remainder, so splitting a total across a payment schedule loses no minor unit), `apply-tax` (exclusive **and** inclusive, `net + tax === gross` always), `line-total`/`summarize` (tax applied ONCE to the summed net, never per line), `format-money` (cached `Intl` per locale+currency+style, `numberingSystem:'latn'` — the locked U8 western-numerals decision) and `parse-money` (Arabic-Indic digits, Arabic decimal/thousands marks, bidi marks).
  - **The test that earned its keep:** `1.005 SAR` must be 101 halalas. `1.005 * 100 === 100.49999999999999` in IEEE-754, so rounding the raw product silently loses a halala on every three-decimal price a human types. `fromMajor` now re-quantises with `toPrecision(15)` before rounding; a regression spec pins it. **This was caught by a spec, not by review** — which is the argument for writing the awkward cases first.
  - **`@sanad/sequence` (19 tests).** `sequence_counter` shared fragment (mounted per service, MS5) + a **single-statement** allocator: `insert … on conflict do update set last_value = last_value + 1 returning last_value`. **Deliberate deviation from the plan's `SELECT … FOR UPDATE`** (`02-mvp.md:135`), recorded so it is never re-litigated: it is one round trip inside a lock the caller already holds, and — decisively — `RepoDb`, what `TransactionRunner.run` yields (N14), exposes **no `execute`**, so raw locking SQL cannot be issued through the seam an application service is allowed to hold. `periodFor` keys on the **UTC** year (the DB writes UTC), and padding is a MINIMUM so the 10 000th proposal of a year is `PRO-2026-10000`, not a truncated collision.
  - **The phase's named acceptance proof, and proof that the proof works.** 20 real concurrent connections, each its own transaction, allocating from one `(entity, period)`: the returned multiset is exactly `1…20` — no gap, no duplicate. Plus the case that defines the word: **allocate → rollback → allocate returns the SAME number**, because the counter moves inside the caller's transaction. Then the assertion was proven to bite — a deliberately non-atomic read-then-write allocator run through the identical harness produced **17 duplicates of 20**. A concurrency test that has never seen the bug it guards is decoration.
  - **`@sanad/audit` (38 tests) — the evidence layer, built before the thing it protects (P8.14).** `audit_log` fragment + **database-enforced** append-only: a `before update or delete` trigger raising `restrict_violation`, shipped as `AUDIT_IMMUTABILITY_SQL` (drizzle-kit cannot generate a trigger, so the constant is the single source every adopting service applies in a `--custom` migration). The store has no `update` and no `delete` **by construction**, so the mutation the trigger refuses cannot even be expressed. Also: `redact` (deny-list on KEYS, cycle- and depth-safe — the audit row is the one place the platform deliberately copies whatever a write contained, which makes it the easiest place for a credential to land in plain text, N13), `diffFields` (only what moved; an added key reads `before: null`, a removed one `after: null`), the ALS-backed `AuditRecorder`, `@Audited()`, and and `@Audited()`, which **binds the interceptor per handler**. (Originally bound via `APP_INTERCEPTOR`; that was WRONG and is corrected in the 2026-08-19 entry — `connectMicroservice` gives the microservice its own `ApplicationConfig`, so no global interceptor reaches RMQ. The guard is also TWO triggers: update/delete per row, plus truncate per statement.)
  - **The trap this design turns on, written down because it is invisible.** An interceptor that calls `als.run(scope, () => next.handle())` opens the scope around _building_ the observable, not around _running_ the handler — `next.handle()` is subscribed after every interceptor has returned, so the handler executes outside the scope and **every actor is silently null**. The scope must wrap the **subscription**. The naive form was written deliberately once to check the specs bite: **5 of 8 failed**, then it was reverted.
  - **Two ways in, on purpose.** `record()` buffers and the interceptor flushes after the handler; **`recordWithin(tx, …)` writes inside the caller's transaction**, so the business row, its Outbox event and its audit row commit or roll back as ONE. Settings uses the latter — proven both ways: a committed write has its row, a rejected write leaves none claiming it happened. And a failed audit write **never** fails the request: the trail records what the product did, it must not become a new way for the product to refuse.
  - **The request-context seam — the gap that blocked everything.** Nothing in the platform captured **ip / user-agent / request-id**: `IdentityContext` is `{userId, roles, permissions, email?}` and the gateway forwarded exactly two RMQ headers. Now `x-sanad-request-context` (contract + schema in `@sanad/contracts`, helpers in `@sanad/messaging` beside `readMessageHeaders`) rides alongside identity (_who_) and locale (_in which language_) to say _from where_. It is captured by an **Express middleware** in the gateway, not a Nest interceptor (same subscription-timing reason), into an ALS scope that `forwardHeaders` reads — chosen over a third parameter because `forwardHeaders` has ~26 call sites and every one now carries the context, so no later phase revisits a route to make `@Audited()` work. `x-forwarded-for`'s **first** hop is the client: reading `req.ip` behind our own nginx would stamp the proxy on every row, which is the same as recording nothing. `requestId` honours an inbound header and is otherwise honestly `null` — **P8.10** generates one and needs no change here.
  - **First adopter: `settings-service`.** `audit_log` mounted at the two points the Outbox uses, migrations `0003` (table) + `0004` (the trigger, body identical to the lib constant), `AuditModule.forRoot()` after `DrizzleModule`, `@Audited({action:'settings.updated'})` on `SETTINGS_SET`, and `SettingsWriter` resolving each key's **current** value before the write so the row records what actually moved rather than what was submitted. Verified in the service's own DB, not just the lib's: `insert` → `update` → **`ERROR: audit_log is append-only: UPDATE is not permitted`** → `delete` → same, row intact.
  - **A shared test-harness fix, because the alternative is a permanent false green.** `infra/postgres/init/01-create-databases.sql` runs **once, on an empty data volume** — so `sequence_test` and `audit_test`, added there this phase, would never exist on any machine whose volume predates them, and both suites would "skip cleanly" forever. `ensureTestDatabase()` in `@sanad/database` creates a `*_test` database if missing (and refuses any name that is not `*_test`, TDD2). Both suites also print **why** they skip — which is exactly how the missing DB was noticed within seconds instead of being read as green.
  - **U26 ratchet: 569 → 566**, struck for the three files this phase touched (`forward-headers`, `settings.writer`, `settings.message-controller`). **A finding worth more than the burndown:** regenerating the baseline **launders a new violation into it**. `settings-writer/settings.writer.ts` does not comply (the rule compares the concept — `settings` — to the directory name), and a blind `--write` silently baselined it as grandfathered. It was caught by diffing the generated list against the previous one. **Never blind-write the baseline; always diff first** — the ratchet protects against forgetting, not against regenerating.
  - **The verifier caught three real things, all fixed rather than argued with.** (1) `@nestjs/microservices` was in `devDependencies` while `audit.interceptor.ts` imports `RmqContext` from it — `@nx/dependency-checks` was right. (2) **`settings.changed`'s `producedAt` in the `EVENT_CATALOG` still pointed at the pre-move `settings.writer.ts` path** — the U26 move invalidated a U27 declaration, and P8.7's `existsSync` assertion caught it. That is the two laws checking each other, exactly as intended. (3) No PROGRESS entry yet (this one).
  - **Deliberately unchanged, so the absence reads as a decision:** `docs/postman/` (DOC2 triggers on a gateway **route** added/changed/removed — this phase adds a header, no route); the `EVENT_CATALOG` (audit is a write-side record with no consumer; inventing an event would only manufacture a 14th declared orphan, U27d); the backend i18n catalogs (money/sequence failures are internal invariants that must not be user-visible, U24f — the first user-facing money message arrives with P8.9's admin surface).
  - **Also considered and not done:** retention/pruning for `audit_log`. A trail you can trim is not evidence; if volume ever forces the question it becomes a partitioning phase, not a delete.
  - **NEXT:** `/continue-plan` → **P8.9 — Legal Entity Profile + configurable money (absorbs P8.6)**, which consumes `@sanad/money`: currency, symbol/placement, tax rate + label and the website's budget bands all become resolved settings with coded defaults, and leads store a stable band code instead of a display label.

- **This session (2026-08-09): P8.7 — the law update + the event catalog — BUILT, verifier PASS. The platform now has one declared event catalog, and both publish paths are typed against it, so an un-catalogued event is a typecheck failure rather than a convention nobody checks. The U26 layout law gained its ESLint rule, shipped as a ratchet per the owner's directive.**
  - **The catalog.** `libs/contracts/src/lib/events/` — `EVENT_ACTORS` (closed union of every producing/consuming surface), `EventTransport`, `EventConsumer`, `EventDefinition`, `defineCatalog()`, and **26 entries** (4 broker + 22 admin client-bus), each a `*.event.ts` declaring its payload schema, producer, `producedAt`, description and consumers. **Three laws are compile-time, not review-time:** (a) `EventDefinition` is a union — either `consumers` is a NON-EMPTY tuple (`orphanReason?: never`) or it is `readonly []` **and** `orphanReason: string`, so `consumers: []` with no reason simply does not compile (U27d); (b) `defineCatalog` constrains the value at key `K` to `{ name: K }`, so a mis-keyed entry fails and a duplicate name is a duplicate object key (TS1117) — "declared twice" is unrepresentable (U3/U27c); (c) `BrokerEventName` narrows `OutboxStore.enqueue` + `createOutboxRecord<TName>(name, payload: EventPayloadOf<TName>)`, and `ClientBusEvent<'admin-portal'>` **derives** the portal's whole `DomainEvent` union. Proven both ways with `@ts-expect-error` probes (which fail the build if the error ever stops happening — the opposite of `@ts-ignore`).
  - **The audit found three real defects, all fixed rather than merely recorded.** (1) `settings.changed` and `user.registered` had **no Zod schema**, and `user.registered` had **no name constant** either — a hardcoded string plus an ad-hoc object literal that nothing could validate. Both now have `.strict()` contracts (`settings/settings-event.zod.types.ts`, `identity/user-event.zod.types.ts` + `USER_REGISTERED_EVENT`) and their producers use them. `settings.changed` also **gained `scopeId`**: the old `{ group, scope }` could not tell a consumer _which_ client's override moved, so the first consumer would have had to drop every cached scope — with zero consumers today this was the only free window to fix it (U0/U28). (2) **`content.service.changed` and `content.blog.changed` were half-wired** — published on every service/blog mutation with nobody listening, while `DashboardCounts.services`/`.blogPosts` are derived from exactly those collections, so the dashboard tiles went stale on every edit. `use-dashboard` now subscribes to both; this is precisely the class of gap U27 exists to surface. (3) **`settings.branding.updated`** was declared in the admin union, never published and never subscribed — deleted, not catalogued (the branding refresh actually rides `settings.updated`).
  - **13 declared orphans, each with a stated reason naming its owning phase** (U27d): `settings.changed` + `user.registered` → **P8.12**; `auth.loggedIn`/`auth.loggedOut` → **P8.11**; the nine remaining `content.*.changed` → **P8.23** (no admin surface outside the owning module reads those collections and `DashboardCounts` does not count them; the website is a separate app and must revalidate over the **broker**, which is a producer-side addition). These rows are the U27 findings that feed those phases — they are input, not paperwork.
  - **Dead code deleted, not preserved.** `libs/shared/messaging/src/lib/domain-event.ts` (+ spec) and `message-pattern.ts` — `defineEvent`/`EventContract`/`EventPayloadOf`/`DomainEvent<T,P>`/`eventPattern`/`requestPattern` were referenced by nothing but their own spec. They could not have hosted the catalog anyway: `@sanad/contracts` is `type:types` and may import nothing internal, so the dependency runs **contracts ← messaging**; keeping both would have exported two different `EventPayloadOf` from two packages (U3). `messaging` gained `@sanad/contracts` as a dependency (`type:util → type:types`, boundary-legal, `@nx/dependency-checks` green).
  - **The U26 ESLint rule — `sanad/u26-logic-unit-folder` in `tools/lint/`.** Decides from **filename + directory name only** (no AST): specs are skipped (which makes U26's `<subject>.e2e.spec.ts` layer-root carve-out automatic); declaration suffixes (`types`/`zod.types`, `constants`, `event`, `table`, `errors`, `permission`, `message-pattern`, …) are exempt; logic suffixes (kept verbatim in step with the verifier's U26 bullet so the two halves cannot disagree) plus `use-*` hooks plus any `.tsx` must sit in a folder named after their concept; and a suffix-less file with a **sibling spec** counts as a proven unit. `normalize()` kebab-cases PascalCase and **strips a leading `use-`**, so both `media-explorer/use-media-explorer.ts` and `use-client-list/use-client-list.ts` pass. Registered in the **ROOT** `eslint.config.mjs` — non-negotiable: lint-staged runs eslint from the repo root, flat config loads exactly one file, and the six backend services have **no** project-level config at all.
  - **The ratchet, and the owner directive behind it.** Owner: _"only feature edits… I know that old work doesn't follow this, no problem; from now let's follow it and fix what's in our way."_ So **no bulk migration phase**. `baseline.json` grandfathers the measured pre-rule tree — **569 files**: 248 `apps/admin-portal`, 122 `apps/website`, 60 content-service, 30 `libs/web`, 22 `libs/shared`, 19 media-service, 19 settings-service, 18 api-gateway, 12 identity-service, 10 `libs/contracts`, 9 notifications-service. Four guards make it one-way: a new violation errors (not baselined); **a baselined path that now complies also errors** ("delete the entry"), so a file a later phase touches is fixed and struck in that same change without anyone remembering to; `baseline.spec.mjs` asserts `paths.length <= maxPaths` (a committed number that only goes down) and that every listed path still exists, is sorted and unique. Regenerate only via `nx run lint-rules:baseline` — the generator lints with the **real rule** and an empty baseline, so generator and enforcer can never disagree.
  - **Two traps worth not re-learning.** (a) **`tools/eslint-rules/` is Nx's, not ours.** `@nx/eslint-plugin`'s `resolve-workspace-rules` claims that exact directory, `require`s a `tsconfig.json` + `index.ts` there, and prints a **stack trace on every single `eslint` invocation** when they are missing — our plugin is deliberately ESM `.mjs` (the root config is `.mjs` and all ten project configs hard-import it by that literal filename), so it lives at `tools/lint/` instead. (b) That move costs the inferred lint input: `@nx/eslint/plugin` hardcodes `{workspaceRoot}/tools/eslint-rules/**/*`, so `nx.json` now sets `targetDefaults.lint.inputs` naming `{workspaceRoot}/tools/lint/**/*` — **without it a burndown commit that only edits `baseline.json` would serve stale cached lint results**. Verified the merged inputs with `nx show project contracts --json`; the per-project `eslint.config.mjs` is still covered because `default` = `{projectRoot}/**/*`.
  - **Also hardened:** a bare `client.emit(...)` in `apps/**` is now a root-config `no-restricted-syntax` error (MS3/U27) — the one Outbox bypass no type can catch, because both the name and the payload would be perfectly legal.
  - **U26 debt paid on everything this phase touched** (the policy demonstrated, not just described): `data/events/event-bus/`, `data/events/use-event-bus/`, `modules/dashboard/hooks/use-dashboard/`, and `messaging/.../outbox/outbox-record/` all moved into concept folders with barrels. All 25 deep imports of `.../data/events/event-bus` resolved unchanged through the new directory barrel — zero call-site churn.
  - **Verified.** `nx run-many -t typecheck lint test build` green with the stack DOWN (OPS5). Both directions proven and reverted: an un-catalogued name in `createOutboxRecord` → TS2345; `publish({ type: 'lead.exploded' })` → TS2322; an orphan stripped of its reason → no union member matches; a duplicated catalog key → TS1117; a `consumers[].at` pointed at a missing file → the catalog spec's `existsSync` assertion fails; a flat `probe.service.ts` → the U26 rule names the exact target path; a compliant path added to the baseline → the ratchet's stale-entry error. **Type proofs must run `--force`/`--skip-nx-cache`** — the `typecheck` target's inputs use the `production` named input, which **excludes** `*.spec.ts`, so a spec-only edit otherwise returns a cached green.
  - **The verifier took THREE passes on one guard, and what it taught is worth more than the guard.** The MS3 bare-publish rule (`no-restricted-syntax` over `apps/**`) was written three times. **Draft 1** matched `callee.object.name` — an **Identifier** — so `this.client.emit(...)`, the form every NestJS publisher actually uses, walked past it. **Draft 2** widened to a tail match derived from the services' `<service>Client` convention — but `apps/api-gateway` injects five proxies named plainly after their service (`content`, `identity`, `notifications`, `settings`, `media`), and **the gateway has no database**, so an `emit` there can never be paired with an Outbox row: the one place the rule could only ever be right was the one place it stayed silent. Computed (`this['x'].emit`) and aliased (`const c = this.x; c.emit`) forms escaped both drafts. **Draft 3 inverts the rule** — every `.emit(` in `apps/**` is a violation unless it is `this.emit(...)` (a store notifying its own subscribers) or `<x>Emitter.emit(...)` (an in-process bus, which N6 permits _within_ a service). Measured: **zero** hits across `apps/**` today, so deny-by-default costs nothing now and closes the door on every handle a future phase injects. **The durable lesson, now in `tools/lint/lint-rules.md`: do NOT select on names.** An allow-list over a name sample re-opens the moment someone injects a new handle, and there is no type backstop — `ClientProxy.emit(pattern: any, data)` accepts anything, so lint is the only line of defence. Where deny-by-default is impossible, enumerate every handle of the type first (`grep -E ': *ClientProxy' apps/*/src` — 26 sites) rather than sampling; and **probe with the shapes that actually appear** — both misses survived review because the spec exercised names appearing nowhere in this codebase. All escapes the verifier found are now committed regression cases (34 tests), the fix was live-probed on `dashboard.controller.ts` (5/5 error) and `r2-storage.provider.ts`, and the fourth pass's residual gaps were closed rather than logged: a second selector catches `client['emit'](…)`, the glob now covers `.tsx` **and `libs/**`** (so a shared lib cannot grow a direct publish path), with one path carve-out for the Outbox relay — **proven load-bearing** by removing it and watching the relay itself error, then restoring it. Zero false positives across `apps/**`+`libs/**`. The verifier also caught the orphan count stated as 11 when the enumeration is 13 (2 broker + 2 auth + 9 content) and an `auth.loggedIn` orphan reason that described a sign-OUT; both corrected.
  - **Considered and deliberately not done:** typing the gateway's injected handles as `Pick<ClientProxy, 'send'>` so a publish there is unrepresentable rather than merely linted. It is a genuinely good idea, but the inverted selector already catches every form including computed and aliased ones, and it would churn ~26 controller constructors for no additional coverage. Recorded as an option for whoever next touches gateway wiring, not as debt.
  - **`docs/postman` deliberately unchanged** — DOC2 triggers on a gateway route being added, changed or removed, and this phase adds none. Recorded so the absence reads as a decision.
  - **boot-verifier: UP** — 13/13 containers healthy, `RestartCount=0`, one `Starting Nest application` per backend (no supervisor loop), gateway `GET /api/health` 200 through nginx, 110 routes still mapped, `/ar` + `/admin/` 200. The N12 risk was the real one here (`libs/contracts` grew a whole new barrel every service imports) and it was proven by **execution**, not by absence of errors: `USER_REGISTERED_EVENT` / `userRegisteredEventSchema` / `EVENT_CATALOG` (26 entries) were imported inside the running identity and settings containers. The seeder short-circuits on an existing admin, so the new `user.registered` write path was exercised against `identity_test` in a rolled-back transaction (TDD2, no dev data touched) — the new three-field payload lands, the old two-field shape is rejected by `.strict()`. The declared orphan is even visible in the broker: `rabbitmqctl list_queues` shows `settings.events` with **0 consumers** while `content.events` and `media.events` have 1 each.
  - **Two infra defects the boot gate surfaced, both fixed here rather than logged.** (a) **`libs-builder`'s readiness marker survived a stack teardown**, so `depends_on: service_healthy` went green on a marker from 2026-07-25: measured **healthy at +9s, build actually finished at +21s** — twelve seconds of services booting against a stale `libs/*/dist`. It was harmless only because the host dist happened to be current; on a cold start after a lib change it is precisely the N12 failure the gate exists to catch. `infra/scripts/dev-libs.mjs` now `rmSync`es the marker before the first build, **proven by restart**: marker absent while the build is in flight, present + healthy only after. **OPS8 amended** with the invariant (not a new rule — U13). (b) `tsc --build` does not prune removed outputs, so `messaging/dist/lib/` still held `domain-event.*` and `message-pattern.*` for the two modules this phase deleted — unreachable through the barrel, but a deep import would have silently resurrected a dead API. Pruned.
  - **NEXT:** `/continue-plan` → **P8.8 — platform primitives: audit, sequence, money** (`libs/shared/audit`, `libs/shared/sequence`, `libs/shared/money`; backend-only, pure TDD; the extra proof is that **concurrent** sequence allocation produces no gaps and no duplicates). Everything it creates is greenfield, so it must be born U26-compliant — the baseline is for the old tree only.

- **This session (2026-08-08, owner-directed PLAN-ONLY: "finish everything necessary to start receiving clients before deploying"): PLANNED — 17 phases written into the spine, 6 new rules into the law, 2 new deep plans. No feature code written (deliberately — the owner asked for a planning session so the next `/continue-plan` can build).** Owner: _"after deploying I will start publish ads to receive clients, so I want to finish anything necessary for that before"_ — plus two named gaps (contracts; "detect what the system misses") and a standing quality directive.
  - **The audit — read from the code, not inferred.** P0–P8 are built, but the **entire commercial half of the product does not exist** and the half that does exist cannot survive ad traffic. **No contracts / proposals / e-sign / documents at all** (zero tables, zero message patterns, zero routes across all six services and `libs/contracts` — grep returns only marketing copy). **No client entity**: `CLIENT_ROLE` exists in `libs/contracts/src/lib/identity/role.ts` but is seeded `[]` in `apps/identity-service/src/seed/seed-data.ts` — a label, nothing more. **A lead is a dead end**: `lead.table.ts` has **no phone**, `leadStatusEnum = ['new','contacted','archived']` set free-form with no guards, no owner, no notes, no activity, no dedup, no conversion; the admin module is a table + a status dropdown. **`POST /api/leads` is public with no `ThrottlerModule`, no helmet, no CORS policy, no captcha, no honeypot** anywhere in `apps/api-gateway/src`. **No password reset, no email verification, no login lockout** (explicitly out of scope in `user-update.zod.types.ts`) — forget the prod admin password and you are locked out of your own product. **No GA4/GTM/Meta/Snap/TikTok anywhere in `apps/website`** — you cannot optimise ad spend without conversion events — and no consent banner. **A failed email stays failed forever** (no retry sweeper; `email_status` goes `pending→failed` and stops). **No audit log, no PDF generation, no sequential numbering** (only `updated_by_user_id` on three integration tables; all IDs are `uuid`). And **two orphan events**: `settings.changed` and `user.registered` are emitted and consumed by **nobody**.
  - **Contract research (web, as the owner asked).** The professional standard is a two-layer **MSA + SOW** model wrapped in a CLM layer: clause library, versioned templates with merge variables, approval workflow, e-signature, tamper-evident audit trail, amendments/change orders, renewal tracking. Saudi **ETL** (Royal Decree M/18; Implementing Regulation 13 Mar 2024) makes e-signatures binding where the signature is uniquely linked to the signatory, under their control, and evidenced — the **DGA-licensed CSP certificate** is a stronger evidentiary tier, kept reachable through a provider seam rather than blocked on.
  - **Four owner decisions (recorded so they are never relitigated).** ① **E-signature in-house**, behind the existing `capability → provider → active` seam (same shape as storage/email), so a licensed CSP is later one adapter and zero UI change. ② **Pre-deploy commercial scope = clients + proposals + contracts**; invoicing/payments are P10, but the contract carries a first-class **`payment_schedule`** so nothing is half-built. ③ **Client portal built NOW** — owner: _"I don't want to delay what I need in deploy after deploy"_ — and explicitly **not** the way the admin portal is organised. ④ **No legal entity is registered yet** (may be Syria, Saudi, or elsewhere) and **the platform may be sold to other tech companies**, so nothing about the company, its jurisdiction, its currency or its tax is hardcoded; the default template is **jurisdiction-neutral** and ships in **`individual` party mode**. _(Stated once and built around: with no registered entity the owner personally is the contracting party and personally liable — a generic template is legal, naming a company that does not exist would not be. Flipping to `company` mode later is a settings change.)_
  - **New law.** **`U0` added verbatim as the FIRST rule** — the owner's 2026-07-30 perfection directive, transcribed unaltered, outranking everything; its operative clause for planning is _a phase is a unit of scope, not of time — split it, never compress it_. **`U27`** is the owner's second directive of the day (_"each action should publish an event bus… so the chain of the event bus is not missing any existing action"_), made checkable by a single **`EVENT_CATALOG`** in `libs/contracts` that every backend and frontend event must be declared in with its producer and consumers — the backfill that writes it will immediately expose the two orphans above. **`W7`** pins the client-portal tree to the `bishola-app-v2`/`TRIPPRO_DASHBOARD` canon. **`U26`** (logic unit = folder + colocated spec), **`U28`** (decide, don't ask for the smaller option), **`U29`** (a feature isn't finished until the _running_ environment runs it) and **`U30`** (never do irreversible work you can't prove is safe; a cache is never a dependency) are **ported from the owner's Aysar rules file at his request**, under **new Sanad IDs** — Aysar occupies `U0–U23`/`N1–N28`/`W1–W9`, so merging by ID was impossible. Aysar's `N4` (single DB + per-domain `pgSchema`) and its in-process QueryBus were deliberately **not** ported: they contradict this platform's DB-per-service + RabbitMQ architecture (MS5, MS2). `U20` gained an amendment pointer to `U26` rather than being rewritten.
  - **A correction worth recording, because it changes what "follow the reference" means.** `TRIPPRO_DASHBOARD` has **no rules doc** (its README links three files never committed — verified against all git history), **zero tests** (no runner, no `@testing-library`, no spec files), and **hooks/services/utils are flat files — only components are folders**. So the law the owner described (_"a component has its file and its test file, both in a separate folder"_) is **not written in TRIPPRO and not implemented there**. It **is** written in Aysar (`U22`+`U18`), whose `W2` states verbatim that its tree was _"transcribed from `bishola-app-v2` and `TRIPPRO_DASHBOARD`"_. Resolution: **Aysar is the law, TRIPPRO supplies the concrete file shapes**, and the law is generalised past TRIPPRO to _every_ logic unit, not only components. Several reference defects are corrected at transcription rather than inherited: untyped `Record<string, unknown>` bus payloads (→ typed per-event map), an envelope built then thrown away, a dead `storeFactory`, menu-only permission gating with routes left open, zero `lazy()`, no error boundaries, and the same `err?.data?.errors → setError` block hand-rolled in three modals.
  - **Written this session:** `docs/rules.md` (U0 first + U26–U30 + W7 + U20 amendment + one dated change-log entry); `docs/plans/02-mvp.md` (**P8.7 → P8.23** inserted before P9, with **P8.6 marked ABSORBED into P8.9** — contracts need configurable money anyway, and doing it twice is the duplication U3 forbids); **new** `docs/plans/08-client-intake.md` (service boundaries, document model, variable catalog, both state machines, the event catalog, the template pack spec, build order + why); **new** `docs/plans/09-client-portal.md` (the W7 tree, the leaf shapes, the event bus, the three reference corrections, the authored test layer); `.claude/agents/verifier.md` (U26/U27/U0-states/U29 checks + "mechanically green but merely working = FAIL").
  - **The 17 phases, in order:** P8.7 law+event catalog · P8.8 audit/sequence/money libs · P8.9 Legal Entity Profile + configurable money · P8.10 gateway hardening + bot defence + `lead.phone` · P8.11 identity completion · P8.12 notifications completion · P8.13 `documents-service` I (engine + **PDF via headless Chromium** — the only engine that shapes Arabic; `@react-pdf/renderer` and `pdfmake` do not) · P8.14 `documents-service` II (e-signature) · P8.15 `crm-service` I (clients) · P8.16 `crm-service` II (proposals) · P8.17 `crm-service` III (contracts) · P8.18 default template pack · P8.19 admin CRM surfaces · P8.20 admin proposals/contracts + structured clause editor · P8.21 client-portal foundation · P8.22 client-portal surfaces · P8.23 website go-live pack · then **P9 deploy**.
  - **NEXT:** `/continue-plan` → **P8.7**. Nothing is checked off; no code was written this session.
  - **Flagged, not silently done:** realigning `apps/admin-portal` to the `W7` tree is a real post-deploy phase (it is ~480 green tests of working product). The owner's instruction was that the _client portal_ must not be built the way the admin portal was — not that the admin portal be migrated now.

- **This session (2026-08-08, owner ASK — "`git push` takes a lot of time… is not moving these tasks to github workflows better?"): DONE, gate re-architected into three layers.** `.husky/pre-push` ran the full `nx run-many -t typecheck lint test build` over all 17 projects on **every** push (Nx task history: 4–8 min cold). Now: pre-commit `lint-staged` → pre-push **`nx affected -t typecheck lint test`** (no `build`) → new **`.github/workflows/ci.yml`** running the complete `run-many` gate on every push to `main` + every PR. Owner decisions: keep pushing straight to `main` (CI **reports**, does not block; `pull_request` wired so branch protection can be turned on later untouched), and cache CI with `actions/cache` over `.nx/cache` rather than reversing `neverConnectToCloud`.
  - **The dangerous find — `nx affected` was going to fail OPEN.** `getBaseRef()` is `defaultBase ?? 'main'`, and we push to `main` _from_ `main`, so `merge-base(main,HEAD) === HEAD`. **Measured: 0 affected projects vs 17 with the correct base** — the slim hook would have passed having checked nothing. Fixed with `"defaultBase": "origin/main"` + the hook reading the real remote sha from the stdin git hands every pre-push (`<local ref> <local sha> <remote ref> <remote sha>`), which is fetched during the push itself, so no `git fetch` and never a stale base.
  - **Two latent bugs the split would have hidden, both fixed.** (1) **`website` had NO `typecheck` target** — Next mandates `noEmit` + `composite:false` so `@nx/js/typescript` never inferred one, and `run-many -t typecheck` silently skipped the entire Next app (its only type safety was `next build`, which is exactly what pre-push no longer runs). New `apps/website/project.json`; it needs `dependsOn: ["^build"]` because `tsconfig.json`'s `references` **redirect every `@sanad/*` import to that project's declaration output** — verified with `--listFiles`: the program is **197 built `.d.ts` and zero lib source**. (2) `sharedGlobals` was `[]`.
  - **Three traps worth not re-learning** (all in the rules change-log): never set `CI=1` to stop vitest watching — `{env:'CI'}` is a hash input on the `test` target, so it forks the local cache; use `testMode:"run"`. `test-ci` is _worse_ than `test` here — each per-file subtask inherits the whole project's inputs, so one edit invalidates all 67 website subtasks, 277 cold starts for zero granularity (it only pays under Nx Cloud distribution, which is off). And `sharedGlobals` reaches only targets that use the `default`/`production` named inputs — `@nx/js/typescript` targets use an explicit file list and are untouched by it.
  - **Verified by measuring, not asserting:** 0-vs-17 affected proven both ways; `website:typecheck` proven to FAIL on a planted error in website source **and** on one in lib source (via `^build`), then reverted; `util:test` proven to drop to a cache miss on a `pnpm-lock.yaml` edit; the hook dry-run exercised across all four ref shapes (normal push / new branch with zero remote sha / branch deletion / unknown remote sha); workflow YAML parsed and step list asserted.
  - **The gate, once it finally ran far enough, exposed a REAL pre-existing bug: `admin-portal:test` died with `JavaScript heap out of memory` — and it had NEVER passed.** It was `◼` cancelled in the owner's original push output, cancelled again in the first gate run, and Nx's task history holds a 1069s outlier for it that had been written off as a stray watch session. Isolated (nothing else running, so not a `--parallel` artifact): 108/109 files pass, `media-explorer.spec.tsx` kills the worker. Root cause was **not** the test — **two copies of `@radix-ui/react-focus-scope` (1.1.10 and 1.1.16) were installed and appeared ALTERNATING in one stack trace**: `@radix-ui/react-context-menu@2.3.7 → react-menu@2.1.24 → focus-scope@1.1.16` vs `react-dropdown-menu@2.1.18 → react-menu@2.1.18 → focus-scope@1.1.10`. Each copy installs its own document `focusin` listener, so scope A refocuses into itself → `focusin` → scope B sees focus outside _its_ scope and refocuses → ∞ → stack overflow → jsdom's `reportException` recurses building the error object → heap exhausted. The spec that triggers it is _"right-clicking a file offers Delete, which asks to confirm"_ — a context menu opening a dialog, i.e. exactly the two components on different copies. **Fix = converge the versions** in `apps/admin-portal/package.json`: `react-dropdown-menu ^2.1.6→^2.1.24`, `react-dialog ^1.1.17→^1.1.23`, `react-popover ^1.1.6→^1.1.23`, `react-select ^2.3.1→^2.3.7`; the lockfile now resolves a **single** `focus-scope@1.1.16`. Bumping dropdown-menu alone was NOT enough — it deduped `react-menu` while dialog/popover/select still dragged the old focus-scope in, which would have kept the loop alive on every dialog-plus-menu screen. Result: **109/109 files, 482/482 tests, exit 0.** **Class of bug to remember: two copies of a focus/scroll/portal-managing library in one tree fight each other via document listeners; the symptom is an OOM or a stack overflow, never a normal assertion failure — and a duplicated transitive dep is invisible until something mounts both at once.**
  - **NEXT:** first push after this lands is still slow (all 17 affected + a one-off cache bust) — expected, not a regression. Then watch that the _second_ push prints a 1–3 project affected set. **If the hook ever prints an empty affected set on a real change, stop — that is the silent-pass, a P0, not a speed win.**
  - (No new rule ID — U13 lean-rules. **OPS4** was restructured and **U12** clarified in place, with the full diagnosis in the change-log.)
- **This session (2026-07-25, owner FIX — "Upload file does nothing, no request sent at all"): FIXED + proven live.** Clicking Upload, picking a file → nothing, not even a network request. Root cause: `onFilesChosen` read `const files = event.target.files` (a **LIVE** FileList) then set `event.target.value = ''` (to allow re-picking the same file) on the next line, which **emptied that very FileList** before the `files.length > 0` check — so `uploadHere` was never called and the upload never started. Fixed by snapshotting `Array.from(event.target.files ?? [])` **before** clearing the value. **jsdom does NOT reproduce this** (it keeps `input.files` after `value=''`), so it was invisible to the whole suite and to typecheck — the same "only breaks live" class as the router-context crash. Drop legality is decided **while hovering** (`evaluateDrop`: same-folder / self / descendant-cycle, mirroring the service's guard), so an illegal target never lights up and the overlay says why — instead of a round-trip 400.

- **This session (2026-07-20, owner-directed CROSS-CUTTING: backend message localization — new rule U24):** the owner tested an R2 connection with the portal in **Arabic** and got `Connected — bucket "bishola-images" is reachable.` in English, and asked the right question: _"what is the status of translations from the backend? this have to be implemented in all services… this is a main rule."_
  - **The diagnosis — not an integrations bug, a platform hole.** `x-sanad-locale` / `Accept-Language` appeared **nowhere** in the repo: the backend had **no locale concept at all**. Every message was a hardcoded English literal — the five services' `RpcException` factories, the gateway guards, `libs/shared/auth`'s `JwtAuthGuard`/`PermissionsGuard`/`@CurrentUser` (the most-hit errors in the product), the shared Postgres error map, the Zod pipe's `'Validation failed'`, `ok()`'s `'OK'`, and the `testConnection` strings — all of which the portal renders verbatim (`transform-error.ts`).
  - **The architecture (owner-decided): keys + translate at the EDGE.** A service never emits prose; it emits `messageKey` + `params` (`rpcError`/`httpError`). The gateway's response interceptor + exception filter render it **once**, in the caller's locale. The locale rides `x-sanad-locale` (→ `accept-language` → `ar`), set by each frontend's single header place and forwarded gateway→service in the RMQ headers beside the identity (MS4) — so text living **inside a payload** (a test result, later an email) is translated in-service from the same locale.
  - **New:** `libs/shared/i18n` (**`@sanad/i18n`**) — `ar`/`en` catalogs (`ar.catalog.ts` is the source of truth; `en` is `Record<MessageKey, string>`, so a missing translation is a **compile error**, with a parity spec on top), `translate()`, `resolveRequestLocale()`, `localeToHeaders()`, `rpcError`/`httpError`. Contracts gained `LocalizedMessage`/`isLocalizedMessage` and `LOCALE_HEADER`/`toLocale`/`resolveLocale` (in **contracts**, not i18n, so browser apps don't pull NestJS into their bundle).
  - **Converted:** all five services' error factories; the gateway (`@RequestLocale`, `forwardHeaders(identity, locale)` at **every** `sendRequest` call site — ~90 handlers across 19 controllers — and a key-preserving `toHttpException` that **drops** un-keyed upstream prose); `@sanad/util`'s interceptor/filter/pipe/Postgres map; `@sanad/auth`'s guards; the admin `base-query` + the website `fetchEnvelope`/lead POST.
  - **The reported bug, fixed properly:** `testConnection(provider, locale)` in media + notifications; `IntegrationTestResult` gained **`detail?`** carrying the vendor's raw English (`SignatureDoesNotMatch`, `ENOTFOUND`) rendered as a muted `dir="ltr"` monospace line under the Arabic message — the owner keeps the diagnostic without the UI leaving Arabic.
  - **Bonus fix:** settings-service's validation failures were `BadRequestException`s thrown inside `@MessagePattern` handlers, which reached the client as **500**; they are `RpcException`s now, so the **400 survives the broker** (the latent bug noted in the 2026-07-19 change-log entry).
  - **Content note:** not-found is **one key per entity** (`content.notFound.blogPost`, `.menuItem`, …) — Arabic can't take an English entity name pasted into a template.
  - **The verifier earned its keep — it took FOUR passes and caught 14 findings I'd have shipped.** Pass 1 (FAIL): the **CORS panel on the very same screen** was still raw English (`corsStatus`/`applyCors` never took a locale) — the owner's bug, one button over; plus the Zod pipe's field-error map was **dead code** (the wire envelope dropped it) and `transform-error.ts` still said `Network error` in English. Pass 2 (FAIL): a **multiline** `new BadRequestException('CORS applies to the storage capability only.')` that single-line greps miss, and a prose Zod message in `branding-update`. Pass 3 (FAIL): the **mechanism itself** — `translate()` returns an unknown key unchanged (by design), so the exception filter's prose fallback still let _framework_ English (`Cannot GET /api/x`, body-parser errors) reach an Arabic caller; plus the team-alert email was English-only. Pass 4: **PASS**. Fixes: `hasMessage()` gates every un-keyed string (filter + pipe + the `errors` values) and un-keyed exceptions now resolve to a status key (`common.notFound`/`.requestFailed`/`.internalError`); `RecordNotFoundError` maps to a real localized **404** (it was silently a 500); the team alert follows a new `NOTIFICATIONS_TEAM_LOCALE` (ar default); `hasMessage` uses `Object.hasOwn`, not `in`, so `'constructor'` can't be mistaken for a key and crash `interpolate`.
  - **Green:** `nx run-many -t typecheck lint test build` — **17 projects, 1429 tests, 0 failures** (the only 2 lint warnings are pre-existing, in `libs/contracts/src/lib/settings/integrations.spec.ts`).
  - **`boot-verifier` UP (13/13, RestartCount 0, 212 routes mapped)** and the localization was proven **over the real wire** through nginx: `/api/no-such-route` → `لم يتم العثور على المطلوب.` (not `Cannot GET …`); `accept-language: en-GB` with no app header → English (precedence); no headers → Arabic; an unauthenticated `/api/media` → `يلزم تسجيل الدخول للوصول إلى هذه الصفحة.` (not `Missing bearer token`); `/api/branding` → `تم` / `OK`, never the raw key `common.ok`.
  - **Verified by looking (U21e).** Drove the real portal at `localhost:8190` in **ar and en**, clicked Test connection, and read the result: Arabic message + `Access Denied` on its own muted `dir="ltr"` monospace line, sidebar correctly on the right, `scrollWidth - clientWidth = 0` (no RTL overflow), 0 page errors. (A `fullPage` screenshot of an RTL page with a fixed sidebar composites oddly — the **viewport** shot is the truthful one; `scrollWidth` is the real overflow signal.)
  - **Law:** **U24** added to `rules.md` with a dated change-log entry, plus the owner's standing directive — **we are building the FINAL product, not an MVP** — recorded in `rules.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `/continue-plan`, this file, and memory.
  - **Open issue (tracked, deliberately not in this change):** the Zod pipe's per-field `errors` map is not carried on the N9 wire envelope (`failHttp` emits only `{success,message,data,path,timestamp}`), so field-level validation keys don't reach the client. Forms validate client-side first (F6), so it is a backstop path — but the envelope should grow an optional `errors` leaf in a follow-up.

  - **The model now** (checked against the industry standard — Laravel `filesystems.php` `default`+`disks`, `mail.php` `default`+`mailers`; Strapi's per-plugin provider + `providerOptions`): **capability → many provider configs → ONE active**, all derived from `INTEGRATION_CATALOG` in `libs/contracts`. Adding a provider (S3/Cloudinary/Anthropic) = **one catalog entry + an adapter**; the capability card, the descriptor-driven config form (reusing `DynamicForm`, F9), the per-capability RBAC key, the identity seed and the Postman entry all follow with **zero UI code**.

New `IntegrationsService` (masked `getStatuses`, no per-key N+1; `configureProvider` where an ABSENT field is left untouched — how a masked secret survives an edit — and `''` clears; `activateProvider`; internal-only `resolveConfig`). Migration `0002` is additive + `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` and **backfilled both live secrets carrying ciphertext verbatim** (same key, no re-encryption). Retired `SecretsService`/repo/controller; the `integration_secret` TABLE is deliberately retained (unused) for rollback.

- **This session (2026-07-19, P8 landing/admin design-bug fixes — owner reported 3 regressions after the P8.5 live-swap): all 3 FIXED + verified by looking (`/ar`+`/en`); `boot-verifier` UP 13/13; `verifier` green mechanically but returned FAIL on ONE pre-existing infra blocker (below) that is NOT from this change set.** The owner reported: (1) the `services` (قدرةٌ كاملة تحت سقفٍ واحد), `testimonials` (ماذا يقول شركاؤنا), and `blog` (ممّا نتعلّمه) chapters had **no container** — full-width/full-height stretch; (2) the `works` chapter (أعمالٌ تتحدّث بالأرقام) cards **clipped mid-card**; (3) the admin Content-Studio **structure rail wouldn't close**.
  - **Root cause #1 (container).** The P8.5 live-swap replaced the website's bespoke `.lp-*` landing sections with the SHARED, width-agnostic `@sanad/web-ui` `.web-*` sections (single source, `mode` prop). Those components deliberately set no `max-width`/padding (each consumer boxes them — the admin canvas does). The website's old boxing (`.lp-shell` 1180px + `.lp-section` padding) was **not re-applied** to the new `.web-*` sections → the grids stretched edge-to-edge inside the tall pinned `.lp-chapter`. **Fix:** website-only CSS restore in `apps/website/src/styles/landing.css` — `.lp-content .web-services,.web-quotes,.web-log-section,.web-stats,.web-cta-band { max-inline-size:var(--lp-maxw); margin-inline:auto; padding-inline:clamp(20px,5vw,72px) }` + box the `.web-works-head`/`.web-orbit-counter` (the orbit ring stays full-width). **Shared component + admin preview untouched** (single source preserved). **Confirmed by looking** (services boxed+centred, works head boxed, `scrollWidth===clientWidth` on `/ar`).
  - **Root cause #2 (works clip), found by probing rects.** `works-orbit.tsx` called `setOrbiting(true)` and created the GSAP `pin: stage` ScrollTrigger **in the same effect pass** — so GSAP read+locked the stage height BEFORE React applied the `is-orbit` class, capturing the pre-orbit `72vh` (648px) clamp and its `overflow:hidden`. The ring (anchored low) placed the front card at 491→987px, but the clip stayed at 648px → the card's bottom ~68% was sliced. Probe proved it (stage computed `block-size:648px` while cards ran to 987px). **Fix:** `stage.style.blockSize='100vh'` set imperatively BEFORE `ScrollTrigger.create` (reset on cleanup) so the pin captures the intended full-viewport stage; **plus** the ring anchor `.web-orbit--live { inset-block-start }` re-tuned 64%→**15%** — the works section renders under the cosmos perspective layer with a constant **+473px** downward offset (only the orbit carries it; the heading doesn't — measured via a rect probe), so 64% dropped the ring below the 100vh stage and its `overflow:hidden` sliced the card; 15% seats the front card fully inside the viewport. Keeps the orbit (owner: "keep orbit, fix clipping"). **Confirmed by looking (U21e):** works front card fully visible + un-clipped across scrub positions on BOTH `/ar` (dir=1) and `/en` (dir=-1); `scrollWidth===clientWidth` (over=0) on every chapter; testimonials + blog + services now render as centred ≤1180px columns (screenshots).
  - **Root cause #3 (rail close).** `StructureRail` is a `Sheet modal={false}` (non-modal, canvas stays live) with **no close button**, outside-click dismissal disabled, no overlay → only the invisible Esc closed it. **Fix:** added a visible `X` close button (`RailClose`, wired to `onOpenChange(false)`) to BOTH the outline + detail `SheetHeader`s; reused the existing `rail.close` i18n key (ar+en parity already present). TDD: 2 new specs (`structure-rail.spec.tsx`) → close from each header calls `onOpenChange(false)`; suite 7/7 green.
  - **Dev-stack note (OPS5/OPS8).** Verifying live hit the known Nx project-graph thrash: rapid edits + reload churn respawned the website `nx dev` and `libs-builder` mid-rewrote `libs/contracts/dist` (transient `Failed to resolve @sanad/contracts`/`No such file` in host vitest + website compile). Recovered per OPS5: `compose rm -sf website` → `docker volume rm sanad-dev_website_nx` → `up -d website`. Also hit a `libs-builder` **SIGKILL mid-build** that left `libs/web/ui/dist` absent → website 500 `Can't resolve '@sanad/web-ui'`; recovered by restarting `libs-builder`.

It returned **FAIL** on 4 items — 3 were mine and are now FIXED: DOC1 `website.md` (container-restore + the corrected `8vh`/`15%` works numbers), DOC1 `pages.md` (the `RailClose` dismissal story), and a `landing.css` comment that still said `33%` while the value was `15%`. `boot-verifier`: **UP** — 13/13 containers, 0 restarts, `/api/health` + all 5 service `/health` 200, `/ar` `/en` `/admin/` 200, and `web-works-track`/`web-services`/`web-quotes`/`web-log-section` all present in the served `/ar` HTML.
  - **⚠ OPEN (pre-existing, NOT from this change — needs an owner decision).** `tsc --build` **silently short-circuits on an mtime inversion**: `libs/contracts/dist/lib/settings/secret-key.zod.types.js` held the pre-R2-wiring registry while `src` had all 7 keys `wired:true`, because the source mtime (07-18 20:22:28) predated the emitted dist (20:23:01) — `nx run contracts:build --skip-nx-cache` did NOT heal it; only `tsc --build --force` did. This made the PREVIOUS session's "verifier PASS / 415 green" record against a dist that did not match src, and admin-portal + backends resolve `@sanad/contracts` through that dist at runtime. **Currently healed** (dist force-rebuilt, now newer than src, `secrets-table.spec` green from a cold cache). Not auto-fixed here because the `build` target is **inferred by the `@nx/js/typescript` plugin** (`nx.json`) with no force flag — guarding it means overriding the build target across all 8 libs (or deleting `dist/tsconfig.lib.tsbuildinfo` as a pre-step), which changes every lib build's incremental behaviour. Decide, then codify.
  - **⚠ Two more escalations from `boot-verifier` (pre-existing).** (1) `infra/scripts/dev-up.sh` **fails under macOS bash 3.2**: `set -euo pipefail` + `"$@"` with no args → `args[@]: unbound variable` (line ~24) before compose runs; fix = `"${@:-}"`. Use the direct `docker compose --env-file infra/.env.development -f infra/docker-compose.dev.yml up -d` meanwhile. (2) The **5 backend services bind `127.0.0.1` inside their containers**, so their published host ports (`3011`…) answer `000` from the host even while healthy — all real traffic goes through the gateway over the compose network, so no runtime impact, but the port mappings mislead host-side debugging.
  - **NEXT** = owner decision on the three ⚠ items above, then back to **P9 deploy** (staging → production).
- **This session (2026-07-18, PRE-P9 dev-runtime hardening — "fix ALL docker development issues so it runs without errors, always"): DONE — the chronic dev-stack fragility is re-architected out, boot-verified end-to-end.** The owner reported the api-gateway "stops after a few seconds" + the website "504 Gateway Time-out". Diagnosed live: the gateway was `Exited(1)` (logs: `Failed running 'dist/main.js'` → Nx `serve → serve` recursive-task wedge; **no `restart:` policy** → stayed dead) → nginx 502/504 → website SSR 500. Six overlapping root causes (see the now-RESOLVED issues below). **Durable fix (new rule OPS8; OPS5 largely superseded):** (1) backend containers run `infra/scripts/dev-entry.sh` → SWC + a chokidar **supervisor** (`dev-serve.mjs`, respawns on crash **and** on shared-dist change) instead of `nx serve` — kills the recursion class + the `node --watch`-stays-dead class; (2) a dedicated **`libs-builder`** container is the **sole writer** of `libs/*/dist` (`dev-libs.mjs`, health-marker gated) — kills the cross-service rebuild cascade; (3) `restart: unless-stopped` + Node liveness healthchecks + readiness `depends_on` on every app container; (4) nginx `proxy_connect_timeout 5s` + `/` read/send `75s` (direct 504 fix) + `/api` `30s`; (5) website read timeout 8000→4000ms (callers already `.catch`→F7). Two watcher traps found by boot-testing + fixed: chokidar v5 **dropped glob support** + Docker Desktop macOS **doesn't deliver native fsevents over VirtioFS** → concrete-dir watching + `usePolling`. New launch scripts `infra/scripts/dev-{up,down,health}.sh` replace the tribal recovery playbook. **Boot-verified:** clean first-boot **13/13 healthy** (libs-builder healthy in 51s → services → gateway → nginx); `curl /api/health`=200, all 5 services `/health`=200, `/ar`+`/en`=200, `/admin/`=200, `POST /api/auth/login`=201 (DI+argon2+pg+RMQ); **kill gateway node PID → supervisor respawns** (never `Exited`, `RestartCount=0`); **`restart content-service` → 0 sibling cascade errors + shared-dist mtimes unchanged**; **gateway stopped → `/ar`+`/en` still 200** (F7 fallbacks, not 500/504); service-src + shared-lib hot reload both confirmed. Files: rewrote `infra/docker-compose.dev.yml` (+`libs-builder`, restart/healthcheck/readiness, dropped backend `*_nx` volumes), 8 `infra/docker/*.Dockerfile` CMDs, new `infra/docker/libs-builder.dev.Dockerfile`, `infra/nginx/dev.conf` timeouts, 6 new `infra/scripts/*`, `apps/website/src/data/http/fetch-envelope.ts`. NEXT = **P9 deploy** (staging → production). Plan file: `docs/plans/before-go-to-deploy-logical-mochi.md`.

About/faq happened to work because their calm Hearth/Beacon systems stay transparent. The green verifier + DOM structural checks (opacity 1, text present, 0px overflow) ALL passed while the band was visually occluded — only element/full-page screenshots caught it. **Fix:** `.mk-cms--hero`/`.mk-cms--tail { position: relative; z-index: 1 }` (+ the bespoke hero's `padding-block-start: clamp(96px,16vh,190px)`), matching `.mk-page`. Verified: all 4 systems now render the hero band legibly over their cosmos backdrop. New rule **WEB12** codifies the architecture. **Built (8 increments, each left the site green):** (1) route accents — `universeSystems` + `pageAccents.contact/ambient` tokens; SSR `AccentStyle` `:has([data-system])` rules off each page's `SystemStage` marker; `WebsiteShell pageAccent="route"` (inline accent omitted); every page (incl. 404/error/CMS catch-all) wrapped in `SystemStage` (+`SystemReady` route-ready signal). (2) the **flight director** (`motion/universe/` — ref-backed store w/ own rAF clock, injectable for tests) + ALL pure flight math TDD-first: `resolve-system`, `resolve-flight-plan` (`'from>to'`→`'from>*'`→`'*>to'`→`'*>*'`), `flight-phases` (idle→departure→cruise→arrival; cruise gate = min-elapsed AND routeReady; `maxHold` force-arrival failsafe; retarget/skip/recovery/reduced-motion + frame-gap clamp all spec'd), `comet-path` (each phase interpolates from the pose the camera was ACTUALLY at — continuous by construction; banks ×`dir`, WEB6), `palette-crossfade`+`voidDip`, `travel-warp`, `travel-content`, `reduce-plan` (WEB4 calm form). (3) **THE one persistent canvas** moved to the locale layout (`UniverseMount`/`universe-canvas` — WEB11 `legacy flat linear alpha:false` flags lifted VERBATIM, cosmos-mount/cosmos-canvas deleted); `UniverseScene` owns the shared pieces (scroll tick at priority −1, Starfield, WarpStreaks, FlightRig, FlightComet, ONE bloom composer) and mounts the active system's scene; `CosmosScene` slimmed to landing-only content (byte-identical journey; **landing regression gate passed by looking** before proceeding). (4) **DOM travel**: `TravelLink` (flight-starting next/link; modified/external/same-place clicks fall through; router-free so it renders in any spec), `PageStage` (`--travel-out/in` + `data-travel-phase`; cruise hard-hides so the streamed page/loading.tsx never flashes), `TravelVeil` (the WHOLE flight on mobile/reduced-motion), `UniverseNavigator` (**push at the departure→cruise boundary** — the old page departs intact, the RSC stream hides in the tunnel; popstate recovery flights; Escape/click skip). (5) **WebGL flight choreography**: `FlightRig` at useFrame priority **0.9** (after every scene camera write, before the bloom composite at 1 — overrides the landing camera with ZERO cosmos edits) drives comet-path camera + fog crossfade (origin→void-dip→destination, sRGB-tagged) + FOV kick + bloom ignition surge; **`travelWarp` max-composed into the scroll store** (`warp = max(scroll, travel)`) so streaks/coin-tail/FOV respond for free; `FlightComet` = the accent-tinted traveler (core + warp-stretched tail). (6) **per-system scenes**: services "**Constellation**" (sky anchor star + 5 worlds on a slow ring + breathing light-lines), serviceDetail "**Close Orbit**" (ONE large indigo ringed planet, ring/moons/spin **seeded from the slug** — every service is its own world; ringGeometry + the landing's strip texture after a plane-vs-annulus fix caught by looking), contact "**Beacon**" (emerald relay + staggered signal rings), ambient "**Adrift**" (CMS/404/error; one far light). (7) **system-lit pages**: detail cover placeholder removed when no image (the planet IS the hero), marketing.css accent-driven hero radials + ONE signature/page (constellation thread · orbital arc · beacon kicker pulse; `overflow-x: clip` guards, calm-mode stilled).   - **Follow-up #2 (2026-07-07, owner vs the reference side-by-side: "strange grey layer over the star background — the design's space is BLACK"): THE recurring grey-space root cause found and fixed — the r128 pipeline needed a THIRD Canvas flag, `linear`.** Bisection with corner pixel-sampling (toggling nebulae → rays → plasma → corona → starfield → halo → star core → coin, each via HMR + headless sample) proved an **empty scene + the bloom pass alone** still rendered `rgb(42,53,75)` — which is **exactly `linearToSRGB(#060912)`**: the near-black clear colour gamma-lifted. Mechanism: the vendored r128 `UnrealBloomPass` renderToScreen path blits the scene through a `MeshBasicMaterial` quad (`UnrealBloomPass.js:202`); r171's default `outputColorSpace = SRGBColorSpace` sRGB-encodes that blit → whole-frame gamma lift (r128's default output was linear → the reference stays black). **Fix: `<Canvas legacy flat linear>`** (`cosmos-canvas.tsx`) — corners return to `(6,9,18)` with EVERY effect restored (plasma, rays, corona, halo, starfield, coin); trust dock verified by looking: black space + the teal gas giant glowing HARDER against it. Along the way the 8.B-4 **nebula backdrop was removed permanently** (owner decision — its additive sprites mottled space grey-green even below the bloom threshold; `nebula-field.ts`/`nebula-backdrop.tsx` deleted, barrel + spec cleaned) and two bisect false-leads documented (hiding `.lp-content` hides the canvas inside it; `#impact{display:none}` NaNs the footer-finale opacity into an opaque overlay). **Gates:** 281 tests green (−3 nebula specs), tsc + eslint clean, WEB11 rules entry added.
